## Document 035: 2026-11-09_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更_65c10ae5

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-09_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更_65c10ae5.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更 
～銀行金利市場化の長い道のり～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹 
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 人民銀がリバースレポ翌日物の日常オペを開始。短期金利の誘導精度を高める狙い 
中国の中央銀行に当たる中国人民銀行（PBOC）が10月30日、市場に短期流動性を供給する「売り戻
し条件付き債券購入（リバースレポ）」翌日物の公開市場操作（オペ）を11月2日より毎営業日実施す
ると発表した。これまで原則として毎営業日実施していたリバースレポ7日物から切り替える。翌日物
のオペは固定金利、数量入札の方式で行い、その金利を政策金利と位置づける。インターバンク市場
のレポ取引では翌日物が大半を占めていることから、政策金利を7日物から翌日物に変更することで
期間のズレを解消し、短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙いがある。新たなオペ初日の金利は1.25％
であった（前営業日のリバースレポ7日物の金利は1.40％）。 
この動きは、PBOCの潘功勝行長（総裁）が10月19日の講演で明らかにしていた金融政策運営の改革
方針を実行に移したものである。潘氏は「銀行間債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）は事実上、中
央銀行の金融政策が調節する目標金利となっているが、それと同時に、中央銀行の公開市場操作の主
要な種類と主要な政策金利の期限はなお7日で、両者は完全には一致しない」ことを認めた上で、「適
切なタイミングで公開市場の主要な操作種類の期限を翌日物に切り替え、公開市場の翌日物レポ金利
を主要な政策金利とすることを検討している」と予告していた。この切り替えによって、PBOCの金融
政策運営は米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）や日本銀行のそれに近づくことになる。 
さらに、潘氏は銀行貸出金利の改革を進めていることも明らかにした。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）を
参照して貸出金利を決定するこれまでの運用に加え、DR001などの短期市場金利を直接参照して金利
を決定する方法を試験的に導入したのである。今後、財務力のある企業は商業銀行と協議の上でDR001
もしくはLPRを参照した貸出金利を選択することができ、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを参照
した金利を適用するとしている。経済誌『財新』によれば、2026年7月以降にDR001を参照した銀行貸
出がすでに全国で100件以上実施されているという。 
PBOCが新たな銀行貸出金利の決定メカニズムを模索する背景には、LPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、
貸出金利の実勢を十分に反映できていないことがあるとみられる。これは、2013年7月に下限規制が撤
廃される前の貸出基準金利が基準の70％まで引き下げを許容していた歴史的経緯から、信用力の高い
顧客にLPR以下の金利で貸出を実行する慣行が残っているためでもある。潘氏は改革を進めると同時
に、すでに実務的な役割を終えている貸出基準金利と預金基準金利を正式に廃止するとした。 
2026 年 11 月 9 日

### Page 2

2

■ 貸出基準金利から最優遇貸出金利へ移行。ただ、実勢金利は「最優遇」から乖離 
PBOCは2010年代以降、銀行金利の市場化改革を慎重に推し進めてきた（図表1）。改革以前はPBOC
が貸出基準金利と預金基準金利を直接コントロールし、商業銀行がその上下一定の範囲内で貸出・預
金金利を決定する制度となっていたが、PBOCは2013年7月に貸出金利の下限規制を撤廃し、同年10月
には全国銀行間コール市場センター（全国銀行間同業拆借中心。以下「コール市場センター」）を通
じて貸出基礎金利（旧LPR）の発表を開始した。旧LPRは、主要9行（のち10行）の報告銀行がコール
市場センターに貸出の最優遇金利を報告し、センター側がそのうち最高値と最低値を除いた報告金利
を報告銀行の貸出残高で加重平均して算出するものであった。 
PBOCは銀行貸出金利が市場原理に基づき決定されるメカニズムへの移行を意図していたが、PBOC
が引き続き基準金利を操作して利下げを行い、報告銀行も基準金利を強く意識して最優遇金利を報告
したため、旧LPRは貸出基準金利に張り付いて変動した。PBOCは「銀行が貸出を実行する際、多くが
なお貸出基準金利を参照して金利を決定し、特に一部の銀行が協調行動を通じて貸出基準金利の一定
図表1 銀行金利の市場化改革 
年月 No. 改革内容 
2004/10  銀行貸出金利の上限規制と銀行預金金利の下限規制を撤廃 
2013/7  ① 銀行貸出金利の下限規制を撤廃（撤廃前の下限は貸出基準金利の 70％） 
2013/10 ② 貸出基礎金利（旧 LPR）の発表を開始 
2015/10 ③ 銀行預金金利の上限規制を撤廃（撤廃前の上限は預金基準金利の 150％） 
2019/8 ④ 貸出市場報告金利（新 LPR）の発表を開始。中期貸出ファシリティ（MLF）金利を参照金利に採用 
2024/7 [1] レポおよびリバースレポ翌日物の臨時オペを開始（金利はリバースレポ 7 日物の▲0.2％と＋0.5％） 
 ⑤ リバースレポ 7 日物金利を LPR の参照金利に位置づけ 
2026/6 [2] レポおよびリバースレポ翌日物の臨時オペを変更（金利はリバースレポ 7 日物の±0.25％） 
2026/11 ⑥ リバースレポ翌日物の日常的なオペを開始。その金利を政策金利に位置づけ

倍率（例えば0.9倍）を隠れた下限金利として設定しており、市場金利が実体経済に伝達することを妨
げている」と指摘している。 
そこで、PBOCは2019年8月、貸出市場報告金利（新LPR）と呼ばれる新たなメカニズムを導入する。
中長期流動性の供給ツールである中期貸出ファシリティ（MLF）の金利を参照し、銀行がこれに資金
調達コストや市場需給、リスクプレミアムを反映した最優遇金利をコール市場センターに報告する方
法である。報告銀行は中小銀行を追加して18行（現在20行）まで拡大し、算出方法も加重平均から単
純平均に変更したことで、算出結果がウェイトの高い大手行の報告金利に引っ張られる傾向を排し、
指標としての包括性も高まった。この改革により、LPRは政策金利であるMLF1年物の金利と連動する
ようになったが、LPRが「最優遇」金利から乖離した状態は依然として解消されなかった。報告銀行
が旧LPRの時と同様にMLF金利を強く意識して金利を報告したためと考えられる。 
図表3は新LPRと実際の銀行貸出金利との関係を見たものである。LPRを下回る金利での貸出比率が
年々高まっていることが分かる。LPR1年物は2019年8月～2026年10月までの間に計▲1.25％Pt引き下げ
られているが、実勢金利は資金需要の低迷もあってそれ以上に下がっていることを示唆している。 
一方、銀行預金金利は2015年に上限規制を撤廃した後も、銀行間の過度な預金獲得競争を防ぐため
窓口指導や業界の自主規制の形をとった上限管理を維持し、2022年4月になって10年物国債利回りや
LPR1年物を参照して金利を決定するメカニズムを導入した。上限管理が廃止されたわけではないが、
預金金利は近年、市場金利の低下に伴って低下傾向にある。 
■ 市場化改革の成果を銀行の実際の金利設定行動に定着させられるかが今後の焦点に 
PBOCは、2019年頃までに政策金利をリバースレポ7日物金利（短期）と MLF（中長期）の 2本立てと
する金融政策運営の枠組みを整え、新LPRの導入でその枠組みを明確化した（月岡（2024）参照）。
2024年7月には、政策金利をリバースレポ7日物に一本化し、LPRの参照金利もMLFからこれに移行さ
せた。中長期金利を市場の決定に委ねる形へと歩を進めたのである。 
PBOCは同時に、誘導目標とする短期金利を一定の範囲内で安定させるため金利コリドー（回廊）の
図表3 新LPRと銀行貸出金利 図表4 金利コリドー

仕組みも整える（図表4）。2024年7月、流動性を吸収するレポと供給するリバースレポの翌日物の臨
時オペを開始し、レポ金利をリバースレポ7日物の▲0.2％Pt、リバースレポ金利を7日物の＋0.5％Ptに
設定することでコリドーの幅を狭めた。さらに、2026年6月には臨時オペの金利をそれぞれ▲0.25％Pt、
＋0.25％Ptに調整し、誘導目標とするDR001が臨時オペ金利の上下限を継続的に逸脱した場合にオペを
実施すると明確化した。リバースレポ翌日物の日常的なオペを実施する準備を整えた形である。 
このように、PBOCは政策金利による短期市場金利の誘導精度を高めるとともに、市場金利の変化を
実際の銀行貸出金利へと円滑に波及させるメカニズムの構築を進めてきた。今回、政策金利をリバー
スレポ翌日物金利に変更し、LPRを介さずにDR001を直接参照する貸出金利の決定方式を導入したこ
とは、一連の市場化改革における重要な節目と位置づけることができる。DR001参照型の金利は指標
金利としてのLPRを代替するものではなく、PBOCは両者を併存させながら金利波及経路を改善するア
プローチを続けるとみられる。今後は、市場化改革の成果を商業銀行の実際の金利設定行動に定着さ
せられるかが焦点となろう。

は否定～」Mizuho RT EXPRESS（7月4日）

四、前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元
10月末，本外币存款余额358.81万亿元，同比增长7.8%。月末人民币存款余额350.65万亿元，同比增长7.7%。前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元。其中，住户存款增加8.47万亿元，非金融企业存款增加1.91万亿元，财政性存款增加3.03万亿元，非银行业金融机构存款
增加5.99万亿元。10月末，外币存款余额1.22万亿美元，同比增长16.9%。前十个月外币存款增加1553亿美元。
五、前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元
10月末，本外币贷款余额287.25万亿元，同比增长4.6%。月末人民币贷款余额283.33万亿元，同比增长4.7%。前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元。分部门看，住户贷款减少1.46万亿元，其中，短期贷款减少1.41万亿元，中长期贷款减少501亿元；企（事）业单位贷款
增加12.39万亿元，其中，短期贷款增加4.65万亿元，中长期贷款增加6.69万亿元，票据融资增加9283亿元；非银行业金融机构贷款减少2395亿元。10月末，外币贷款余额5831亿美元，同比增长5.2%。前十个月外币贷款增加381亿美元。
六、10月份银行间人民币市场同业拆借月加权平均利率为1.3%，质押式债券回购月加权平均利率为1.33%
10月份银行间人民币市场以拆借、现券和回购方式合计成交167.98万亿元，日均成交9.33万亿元，日均成交同比增长1.9%。其中，同业拆借日均成交同比下降10.8%，现券日均成交同比增长4.7%，质押式回购日均成交同比增长1.3%。10月份同业拆借加权平均利率为1.3%，
比上月低0.1个百分点，比上年同期低0.09个百分点；质押式回购加权平均利率为1.33%，比上月低0.07个百分点，比上年同期低0.07个百分点。
七、10月份经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元
10月份，经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，其中货物贸易、服务贸易及其他经常项目分别为1.43万亿元、0.47万亿元；直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元，其中对外直接投资、外商直接投资分别为0.21万亿元、0.35万亿元。

2026年10月金融数据

2026年11月13日，人民银行发布2026年1-10月金融统计数据报告。数据显示，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长
4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。
一、社会融资规模存量同比增长7.2%
初步统计，2026年10月末社会融资规模存量为469.16万亿元，同比增长7.2%。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款余额279.31万亿元，同比增长4.6%；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币余额1.26万亿元，同比增长9%；委托贷款余额11.53万亿元，同比增长1.7%；信托
贷款余额4.49万亿元，同比下降0.6%；未贴现的银行承兑汇票余额2.05万亿元，同比下降4.4%；企业债券余额36.92万亿元，同比增长9.6%；政府债券余额106.41万亿元，同比增长14.4%；非金融企业境内股票余额12.77万亿元，同比增长5.5%。
二、前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元
初步统计，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款增加10.91万亿元，同比少增3.61万亿元；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币增加2546亿元，同比多增3692亿元；委托贷款增加2084亿
元，同比多增1002亿元；信托贷款减少1798亿元，同比多减3958亿元；未贴现的银行承兑汇票减少939亿元，同比多减1055亿元；企业债券净融资3.1万亿元，同比多1.27万亿元；政府债券净融资11.49万亿元，同比少4592亿元；非金融企业境内股票融资5705亿元，同比多
1842亿元。
三、广义货币增长7.1%
10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。

## Document 101: 2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/2 
2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の 73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業 175 社の 2026 年年末

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

## Document 105: 2026～2028年度経済見通し（26年11月）

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026～2028年度経済見通し（26年11月）.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-11-17

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

民間消費 設備投資 公的需要 外需 その他
実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移(年度）(前年比）
(資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
(年度）
予測

＜実質成長率：2026年度 0.9％、2027年度 1.3％、2028年度 1.1％を予想＞

１. 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、民間消費の落ち込みを主因として前期比年率▲0.1％と 4
四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。 
２. 物価上昇ペースの加速に伴う実質購買力の低下を主因として民間消費が低迷することな
どから、2026年 10-12月期、2027年 1-3月期の実質 GDP成長率は 0％台前半から半ばに
とどまることが予想される。2026年度後半の日本経済は低空飛行が続く可能性が高い。 
３. 2027年度に入ると、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下から家計の実
質購買力が改善し、民間消費が回復に向かうほか、原材料価格の低下に伴う企業収益の
改善を受けて設備投資の増勢ペースが加速することが見込まれる。 
４. 実質 GDP成長率は 2026年度が 0.9％、2027年度が 1.3％、2028年度が 1.1％と予想する。
消費税率引き下げによる実質 GDPへの影響は 2026年度が▲0.1％、2027年度が 0.3％と
試算する。 
５. 消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品を除く総合）は輸入物価の上昇が食料、日用品など幅広い
品目に波及し、2026年度末にかけては 3％台まで加速するが、飲食料品の消費税率引き
下げを受けて、2027年度には 1％台まで鈍化するだろう。消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品
を除く総合）は、2026年度が 2.4％、2027年度が 1.5％、2028年度が 2.0％と予想する。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-11-17

2026～2028年度経済見通し（26年 11月）

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

（中東情勢の影響） 
中東情勢の緊迫化を受けて一時大幅に減少した日本の原油輸入量は、代替調達の進展によって持
ち直し、7月以降は前年の水準を上回って推移している。 
財務省の「貿易統計」で原油輸入の動向を確認すると、2026年 3月は攻撃前にホルムズ海峡を通
過・出港していた原油が日本に到着したため、影響はまだ限定的であったが、4、5月には中東産を
中心に原油輸入量が前年比で 6割前後の大幅減少となった。その後、ホルムズ海峡を経由しない中
東産に加え、米国を中心とした代替調達が進んだことから、6月には減少幅が大きく縮小し、7月
以降は、原油輸入量が前年の水準を上回っている。2025年時点で中東産が約 7割を占めていたナフ
サについては、中東からの輸入量は減少が続いているものの、米国、ペルー、アルジェリアからの
輸入量が急増するなど代替調達が進展している。

＜原油＞ ＜ナフサ＞

### Page 3

原油輸入に占める中東の割合（数量ベース）は 2025年時点で 94.0％だったが、足もとでは 60％
台まで低下し、米国の割合が 2025年の 3.8％から 30％台まで上昇している。また、ナフサ輸入に占
める中東の割合は 2026年 5月に 10％まで低下した後、7月以降は 50％前後まで持ち直している。
ただし、2025年の 73.8％と比べれば依然として低い水準にある。

価格面では、ドバイ原油の市況価格は 3月に 1バレル 130ドル近く（月中平均）まで急上昇した
後、6、7月には 80ドル前後まで低下した。その
後、8月に 80ドル台後半、9、10月には 110ドル
程度まで急上昇したが、11月には 100ドルを割り
込んでいる。一方、通関（入着）ベースの原油価
格は、市況価格の変動が遅れて反映されるが、3
月の 1バレル 60ドル台から 6月に 117ドルまで急
上昇した後、7月も 115ドルと高止まりした。8
月には 102ドル、9月には 101ドルまで低下した
ものの、市況価格が 6、7月に大きく低下したに
もかかわらず、通関価格は 9月まで 100ドルを上
回る高水準が続いている。 
原油の輸入量が前年の水準を回復する中、輸入価格が大幅に上昇したため、原油の輸入金額は 6
月に前年比で増加に転じた後、高い伸びが続いており、貿易赤字の拡大要因となっている。 
もともと、通関ベースの原油価格は、指標価格に調整金、タンカー運賃、保険料などが上乗せさ
れているが、中東情勢緊迫後は調整金、タンカー運賃が大幅に上昇したこと、代替調達先の米国か
らの輸入価格が中東を上回っていることなどから、市況価格との差が大きく拡大している。代替調
達が進展するもとでは、原油の輸入価格が市況価格ほど低下せずに貿易収支の悪化につながること
が見込まれる。

（AI関連財が輸出を牽引、交易条件の悪化を緩和） 
グローバルな AI関連需要の拡大を背景に、日本の輸出も AI関連財が牽引役となっている。財
務省の「貿易統計」をもとに、AI関連財1とそれ以外の財に分けて輸出動向を確認すると、輸出数
量全体はそれほど強くないものの、AI関連財は 2026年入り後、前年比二桁の高い伸びが続いてい
る。2026年 7-9月期の輸出数量は、AI関連財が前年比 13.7％と大きく増加した一方、非 AI関連財
は同 1.2％の小幅な増加にとどまった。AI関連財が輸出数量全体を下支えする構図となっている。 
AI関連財は数量だけでなく、価格の上昇も顕著となっている。世界的な AI投資の拡大に伴い、
半導体や電子部品などの需給の引き締まりがその背景にあると考えられる。2026年 7-9月期の AI
関連財の輸出価格は前年比 24.3％と大幅に上昇し、輸出数量の増加と合わせて輸出金額は同 38.0％

1 AI関連財は、WTOが公表する AI関連財（AI-Enabling-Products）103品目。2025年の輸出全体に占める AI関連財の割
合は 17.1％。

＜非AI関連財＞ ＜AI関連財＞

こうした AI関連財を中心とする輸出価格の上昇は、中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う交易条件の悪化を
緩和する役割も果たしている。原油をはじめとする資源価格の上昇は、輸入物価の上昇を通じて交
易条件を悪化させるが、今回は輸出物価も大幅に上昇している。このため、輸出物価を輸入物価で
除して求めた交易条件指数の悪化は比較的小幅にとどまっている。 
過去の資源価格高騰局面と比較すると、2005～2008年頃の世界的な資源価格高騰局面には、原
油などの輸入物価が大幅に上昇する一方、輸出物価の上昇がそれに追いつかず、交易条件が悪化し
た。また、2020年以降も、コロナ禍からの世界経済の回復に伴う資源価格の上昇に加え、2022年
のロシアによるウクライナ侵攻を受けた資源価格の急騰や円安の進行によって、交易条件が大幅に
悪化した。両局面ともに交易条件の累積的な悪化幅は▲30％以上となった。これに対し、2026年
1-3月期を起点とした 7-9月期までの 2四半期の悪化幅は▲6.6％である。AI関連財を中心とする輸
出価格の上昇が、原油価格の高騰に伴う輸入物価上昇の影響を一部相殺し、交易条件の悪化を緩和
している点が、過去の資源価格高騰局面との大き
な違いである。 
ただし、輸入物価の上昇率は輸出物価の上昇
率を上回っており、交易条件の悪化が続いている
ことに変わりはない。今後も原油価格が高止まり
する場合には、海外への所得流出を通じて国内経
済を下押しする可能性がある。一方、AI関連需
要の拡大を背景とした輸出価格の上昇が続けば、
交易条件の悪化を和らげる要因となるだろう。

（2027年の春闘賃上げ率は前年を若干上回る見込み） 
名目賃金を消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）で割り引いた実質賃金は、2026年 1月から
9ヵ月連続でプラスの伸びを維持している。名目賃金（現金給与総額）が 2025年中の 2％台前半か
ら 2026年入り後には 3％台へ伸びを高めたことに加え、政府によるエネルギー負担緩和策の影響な
どから、物価上昇率が大きく縮小したことが実質賃金の押し上げに寄与している。

消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）

2027年の春闘を取り巻く環境を確認すると、有効求人倍率が 1倍を上回り、失業率が 2％台半ば
で推移するなど、労働需給は引き締まった状態が続いており、企業収益は中東情勢緊迫化の影響を
受けながらも過去最高水準の更新を続けている。消費者物価は、政府によるエネルギー負担緩和策
の影響で 2026年入り後 1％台の伸びが続いていたが、9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となり、今後上昇
ペースが加速することが確実となっている。 
賃上げの環境を過去と比較するために、労働需給（有効求人倍率）、企業収益（売上高経常利益
率）、物価（消費者物価上昇率（除く生鮮食品））について、過去平均（1985年～）からの乖離幅を
標準偏差で基準化してみると、3指標の合計は物価上昇率の鈍化を主因として前年を若干下回って
いるものの、企業収益のプラス幅が大きく拡大していることから、全体としては引き続き高水準と
なっている。賃上げの環境は引き続き良好と判断される。

2. 実質成長率は 2026年度 0.9％、2027年度 1.3％、2028年度 1.1％を予想

（2026年度後半は低成長が続く見通し） 
2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の主因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0.6％とマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。2027年
1-3月期は、物価上昇率が 3％台まで高まることに加え、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げ前の買い控
えの影響もあり、民間消費は前期比でマイナスとなる可能性が高く、実質 GDP成長率は前期比年
率 0.2％とほぼゼロ成長にとどまるだろう。2026年度後半の日本経済は物価高による民間消費の低
迷を主因として、低空飛行が続くことが予想される。

予測

（消費税率引き下げの影響） 
政府は 2027年 4月から 2年間に限り、飲食料品（酒類、外食を除く）の消費税率を現在の 8％か
ら 1％に引き下げるとともに、1％相当の範囲内で中低所得者向けの給付を先行導入する予定として
いる。 
食料品の消費税率を 8％から 1％に引き下げると、家計の負担は年間 4.3兆円程度軽減される。こ
れにより期待されるのは、物価の低下、個人消費、実質 GDPの押し上げである。ニッセイ基礎研
究所のマクロモデルによる機械的な試算によれば、食料品の消費税率を 8％から 1％に引き下げた
場合、消費者物価（総合）は▲1.4％低下し、個人消費は 0.7％、実質 GDPは 0.5％押し上げられる。 
ただし、これは消費税率の引き下げ分（7％）が全て値下げに反映された場合の試算である。こ
れまで日本では消費税率引き上げ時にほぼ 100％価格転嫁されていたが、引き下げ時にそれが当て

（家計の実質可処分所得は依然としてコロナ禍前を下回る） 
2026年度入り後の家計消費の低迷は、高校授業料や小学校給食費の無償化による政府消費への振
り替え、豪雨・長雨などの天候不順の影響
もあるが、より長い目でみれば、実質可処
分所得の伸び悩みが消費低迷の主因である。
家計の実質可処分所得（季節調整値）はこ
のところ持ち直しているが、依然としてコ
ロナ禍前（2019年平均）の水準を若干下回
っている。

者報酬は物価高による実質ベースの低下が

### Page 9

（恒常的な所得に連動する家計消費） 
実質家計消費と実質可処分所得は、平常時には連動性が非常に高い。しかし、コロナ禍で一人当
たり 10万円の特別定額給付金が支給された際は、厳しい行動制限が課せられていたことから貯蓄
率が急上昇し、両者が大きく乖離した。また、2024年に所得税・住民税減税が行われた際も可処分
所得の増加は消費の押し上げにほとんど寄与しなかった。 
このことは、消費の持続的な回復を実現するためには、一時的な所得の増加ではなく、恒常的な
所得の増加が重要であることを示している。 
実質可処分所得は、名目賃金の高い伸びが続く一方、物価の上昇ペースが加速することから、2026
年度末にかけて低迷し、消費も停滞することが予想される。2027年度に入ると、食料品の消費税率
引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下によって実質
可処分所得が押し上げられ、消費も伸びを高め
るだろう。ただし、消費税率の引き下げが 2年
間の時限措置となっているため、実質所得の増
加のかなりの部分は貯蓄に回ることが想定され
る。このため、実質可処分所得の増加に比べて
家計消費の増加は小幅にとどまる公算が大きい。
家計貯蓄率は足もとの 1％程度から 2027年度に
は 2％台まで上昇するだろう。 
民間消費は 2026年度が前年比 0.0％、2027年
度が同 1.4％、2028年度が同 0.9％と予想する。

（設備投資は回復するが、供給制約が抑制要因に） 
2026年 7-9月期の設備投資は前期比 1.6％の高い伸びとなったが、4-6月期に特許権の海外譲渡に
伴う研究開発投資の減少によって落ち込んだ反動によって押し上げられた面がある。設備投資は回
復しているものの、企業収益が過去最高の更新を続けていることを考慮すれば、そのペースは緩や
かなものにとどまっている。 
日銀短観などの設備投資計画の強さに対し、GDP統計の設備投資が弱く見える理由のひとつは、
設備投資計画は名目（金額）で、GDP統計の設備投資は設備投資デフレーターで割り引いた実質で
みることが多いことである。名目設備投資は 2020年 4-6月期の 94.1兆円（季節調整済・年率換算
値）を底に着実に増加し、2026年 7-9月期には 135.4兆円となり、この間の伸びは 43.8％となって

### Page 10

また、企業の設備投資意欲は強いものの、人手不足などの供給制約により工事の進捗が遅れてい
ることも設備投資の抑制要因となっている可能性がある。機械受注残高と建設工事の手持ち工事高
を、それぞれ販売額、施工高の何ヵ月分に相当するかでみると、いずれも長期的に増加傾向にある。
高水準の受注残高は先行きの設備投資拡大を示唆すると同時に、人手不足などの供給制約により機
械の生産や建設工事が進まず、計画に比べて実際の設備投資の進捗が遅れている可能性も示してい
る。

設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報
関連投資、AI・半導体関連等の成長分野への投資、Eコマース拡大に伴う建設投資などの案件を軸
に、先行きも増加傾向が続くことが予想される。ただし、資材価格の高騰や人手不足による工事の
遅れから、企業収益の高い伸びや設備投資意欲の強さに比べればそのペースは緩やかにとどまる可
能性が高い。 
設備投資は 2025年度の前年比 2.2％から 2026年度に同 1.0％と減速した後、2027年度が同 2.8％、
2028年度が同 2.7％と伸びを高めると予想する。

（物価の見通し）

以来、3年 10ヵ月ぶりの 2％割れとなった後、1％
台の伸びが続いていたが、9月には同 2.1％と 9
ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となった。 
コア CPI上昇率が 1％台で推移していたのは、
主として補助金政策によって物価上昇率が押し
下げられているためである。ガソリンや電気・
ガス代等の負担緩和策、教育無償化政策などの
特殊要因は 2025年 12月以降、物価上昇率の押
し下げ要因となっており、2026年 9月の押し下

### Page 11

食料（生鮮食品を除く）は 2025年 7月の前年比 8.3％をピークに鈍化傾向が続き、2026年 8月に
同 2.7％となった後、9月には同 2.9％と 14ヵ月ぶりに上昇率が拡大した。帝国データバンクの「食
品主要 195社価格動向調査」（10/30公表）によれば、2026年（1～12月）の飲食料品の値上げ品目
数は 2万 327品目となり、前年同時期の 2万
580品目を若干下回っている。中東情勢悪化
に伴う原材料価格の高騰を受けて、先行き 3
ヵ月の値上げ品目数は 2026年 6月以降、前年
比で増加に転じているが、そのペースはやや
鈍化している。消費者物価の食料（生鮮食品
を除く）は、上昇率が高まるものの、米価格
の下落が当面続くこともあり、2025年夏場に
比べればそのペースは緩やかにとどまること
が見込まれる。 
一方、エネルギー価格は、政府による負担緩和策によって抑制されてきたが、電気・ガス料金へ
の支援策は 2026年 9月使用分（消費者物価への反映は 10月）でいったん終了した。2026年 12月
使用分からは支援策が再開されるものの、補助金額が前年を若干下回ることに加え、2026年春以降
の原油価格高騰の影響が燃料費調整単価に遅れて反映されることから、今冬の電気・ガス料金は前
年を大きく上回ることが見込まれる。さらに、政府は、ガソリン価格の高騰を抑制するため、2026
年 3月から 11月まで、補助金によってレギュラーガソリンの全国平均小売価格を 1リットル当た
り 170円程度に抑えてきたが、12月以降は補助金を段階的に縮小し、価格抑制の基準価格を毎月 5
円ずつ引き上げる方針を示した。このため、エネルギー価格は 2026年 9月に前年比▲0.6％から上
昇ペースが急加速し、2027年入り後には前年比で二桁の伸びとなり、コア CPI上昇率への寄与度は
1％を超えるだろう。 
今回の見通しでは、電気・ガス料金への支
援策については、2027年夏以降も補助金を段
階的に縮小しながら継続し、ガソリン補助金
については、価格抑制の基準価格を 2027年 2
月に 1リットル当たり 185円まで段階的に引
き上げた後、同水準で据え置くことを前提と
している。

聯強(2347)：資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢
不變 
建議：維持買進

一、聯強為亞太地區最大 3C 通
路商，營收成長以半導體業務最
佳  
 聯強為 3C 電子產品之銷售、配送、維修、量身訂做即時生產之專業通路商，為
亞太地區 3C 通路商龍頭，2026 年前三季產品比重分別為商用加值佔 31%、資
訊消費佔 17% 、 半 導 體 佔 48% 、 手 機 產 品 佔 4% ，成長主要來商用加值
(+112%YoY)和半導體業務(+162%YoY)。聯強海外市場包含中國、香港、紐澳、
泰國、印尼、印度及越南等地，其中以中國、台灣和印尼等區域較佳，營收分別
為 YoY+109%、YoY+78%和 YoY+18%。聯強競爭優勢為透過其四合一經營模
式，搭配其綿密的通路發揮規模經濟，並提供高附加價值的後勤運籌(庫存管理
與快速維為修服務等)，於中國主要競爭對手為神州數碼、天音通信控股。

二、半導體業務成長性佳，3Q26 
EPS 2.93 元創新高並優於預期 
 3Q26 營收 2366 億元(+18%QoQ；+136%YoY)優於預估值 14%，其中商用加
值和半導體營收分別為+116%YOY 和+273%YoY，因秉棄部分利潤不佳專案，
毛利率 3.49%優於前季之 3.4%，營業利益 61.41 億元(+28%%QoQ)優於預估
值 24%，因營運資金需求以及籌資成本上升致使利息費用提高至 9.34 億元
(+106%QoQ)，稅後純益 48.83 億元(+25%QoQ；+90%YoY)，EPS 2.93 元優
於預期之 2.5 元並創新高。

三、資金成本上揚導致業務拓展
較為謹慎，估計 4Q26 EPS 2.34
元 
 由於資金成本上揚致使公司放棄部分利潤不佳半導體業務，十月營收月減 34%，
估計 4Q26 營收 2046 億元(-14%QoQ；YoY+62%)，因產品組合變化轉佳，毛
利率提升至 3.58%，稅後純益 38.99 億元(-20%QoQ；+41%YoY)，EPS 2.34
元。

四、AI 長線需求有助商用加值和
半導體業務成長性 
 集邦 預估 2026 和 2027 年全 球九 大 CSP 資 本支 出分 別為 0.95 兆 美元
(+106%YoY)和 1.53 兆美元(+60%YoY)，且 AI 需求從雲端擴展到企業端，其中
以商用加值和半導體業務受惠幅度最大。 
在商用加值業務方面，在台灣和中國資料中心產品出貨挹注下，3Q26 營收
+116%YoY。展望 2027 年，中國十十五規劃和國產化政策推動政府和國企加速
採用本土品牌，推升聯強代理華為、浪潮等品牌業務成長性，台灣則是接獲數個
半導體大廠 AI 基礎建設專案，加計中小企業 AI 建置需求逐步顯現，商用業務將
為營運成長主要動能之一。 
在半導體業務方面，缺貨和漲價效致使記憶體、SSD 和硬碟業務為半導體業務
中 成 長 性 最 佳 者 ， 且 AI 推 論 亦 增 加 CPU 需求，3Q26 半 導 體 業 務 營 收

公司拜訪快報 
YoY+273%，半導體業務為營運成長另一重點業務，但因資金成本上揚，公司將
摒棄部分利潤未達目標專案，此將影響短期營收動能。 
受惠於出貨 ASP 增加，以及原廠擴大促銷力道挽救銷售量，3Q26 資訊家用和
手機業務分別+21%YoY 和+33%YoY。記憶體大漲致使原廠逐步反應成本於 PC
和手機新機售價，並將導致消費者延長換機週期，2027 年此兩塊業務成長性仍
相對保守看待。

五、資金成本上揚將致使接案更
為謹慎，但長期營益率好轉方向
不變 
 聯強今年接獲數個大型 AI 基礎建設專案，致使營收動能以及營運資金需求大增，
3Q26 遇到資金成本上揚，致使利息費用較前季倍增，公司將透過聯貸、可轉債
以及現增降低利息負擔，預期三項籌資工具皆完成後可望降低利息費用 20-
30%。展望 2027 年，儘管營收成長性不如 2026 年亮眼，但接單策略轉趨謹慎
將致使毛利率下滑壓力降低，且預期營業費用增幅有限下，營益率持續好轉方向
不變。

六、維持買進理由  1)預估 2026 和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 9.38 元和 11.07 元。 
2)儘管資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢不變，維持買進
評等，TP 100 元 (2027 PE x 9)。

### Page 1

E Ink Holdings

Cuts 2026 guidance again; PTFE mass 
production in 2028; reiterate Neutral

Reiterate Rating: NEUTRAL | PO: 148.00 TWD | Price: 140.00 TWD

Cuts ’26 sales guidance to +9% YoY; IoT as key ’27 driver  
Mgmt. cut its 2026 guidance again and now expects overall sales to +9% YoY (prior: up 
10-15% YoY; Jan-Oct sales +4% YoY). It expects IoT sales up 35%+ YoY (BofAe: +39% 
YoY, with ESL sales +32% and signage sales more than doubling) and CE sales -15% YoY. 
Mgmt. cited weaker CE demand and still expects signage at single-digit% of 2026 sales 
(BofAe: 7%). The latest full-year guidance implies a mid- to high single-digit% MoM 
decline in Nov and Dec. Mgmt. gave no numeric 2027 guidance, but it still sees ESL and 
signage (especially mid-size) as the key drivers, while CE order visibility stays low. 
PTFE in focus on 3Q26 call; mass production likely in ’28 
E Ink’s PTFE collaboration with Taiflex (8039 TT; not covered) was one of the main 
topics on the 3Q26 call. We now take a neutral view on E Ink’s PTFE business. We see its 
strong R&D capability in material modification and strength in roll-to-roll manufacturing, 
but mgmt. said the business model is still under discussion and mass production could 
occur in 2028. As a result, our estimates currently do not include any PTFE contribution. 
3Q26 recap: less-favorable mix weighs on margins 
OP was 21%/17% below BofAe/consensus as GM/OPM of 52.2%/26.6% came in lower 
than expected (6.1ppt/7.0ppt below BofAe). Mgmt. said the weaker GM came from a 
higher mix of lower-margin module business. NP was 20%/15% below BofAe/consensus. 
Reiterate Neutral; cut PO to NT$148 on lower P/E 
We cut our 2026E EPS by 8% but lift our 2027-28E EPS by 0-5% to reflect 3Q26 miss 
and the latest guidance. We trim our PO to NT$148 (12x 2027E EPS) from NT$185 (15x 
2027E EPS), with a lower multiple on reduced CE demand visibility and macro risk. 12x is 
similar to ESL system integrators’ P/E average. We reiterate our Neutral given moderate 
sales growth, still-expanding margins, and fair valuation (Exhibit 4).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Electronics

Company Description 
E Ink is top global e-paper supplier with over 90% market 
share. E Ink was originally spun out of MIT Media Lab in 
1997 and was acquired by Prime View International in 
2009. The two companies were combined/branded as E Ink 
into e-paper supply chain. Besides its headquarters in 
Hsinchu (Taiwan), E Ink has facilities in Linkou (Taiwan), 
Yangzhou (China), and South Hadley (US).

Investment Rationale 
We have a Neutral rating on E Ink given moderate 2026 
guidance and 2027 sales growth with potential 
demand/order risk due to component shortage and macro 
uncertainty. Yet we still like E Ink's dominant position in 
global e-paper market with over 90% market share, high 
margins thanks to higher mix from better-margin IoT 
business, and positive OCF/FCF.

OBAL RESEARCH

Price objective basis & risk 
E Ink Holdings (PVWIF) 
We derive our PO of NT$148 from 12x 2027E EPS. Our target P/E multiple is set at the 
average of global key ESL system integrators. It is also at the lower end of 11-34x P/E 
range since AI era from 2023. We believe E Ink's valuation has been re-set with a lower 
premium after AI as its business is less directly exposed to AI.

Upside risks: (1) stronger market demand in consumer electronics, (2) faster ESL 
demand/adoption, (3) faster adoption and more applications in various vertical markets 
like smart transportation/city and smart medical, (4) more favorable USD/RMB 
movement to drive margin expansion and/or non-operating items, and (5) higher non-
operating royalty income due to stronger TFT-LCD demand.

Downside risks: (1) much weaker-than-expected consumer demand, (2) slower-than-
expected ESL demand/adoption to replace from conventional labels, (3) lower utilization 
rate to weigh on margins under higher capex and depreciation costs, (4) higher raw 
material cost and/or logistics cost, (5) lower non-operating royalty income due to weaker 
TFT-LCD demand, and (6) unfavorable FX movement (USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) to 
weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1%yoy), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%yoy, OPM 5.8%), 지배이익 1.29조원(-9.8%yoy)을 기록, 도매 판
매 증가로 인한 매출 증가에도 불구하고 인센티브/원자재 비용 증가로 인해 
컨센서스를 하회하는 실적 쇼크를 기록 
 영업이익 증감 요인: 인센티브&가격 -7,270억원, 믹스 -4,650억원, 재료비 
-3,680억원으로 총 1.56조원 비용 발생. 증가 요인은 판매량 +4,420억원, 
환율 +5,290억원, 기타비용 절감 +4,130억원으로 1.38조원 상쇄. 인센티
브로 인한 비용 증가 예상외로 컸는데 EV 판매량 증가 영향. 기말환율 하락
으로 판매보증충당금은 전년비 9,254억원 감소 
 전동화 믹스 증가: 글로벌 xEV 소매 판매 30만대(+47%yoy), 판매비중 
36.2%(+9.8%p yoy). HEV 17.2만대(+46%yoy), 텔루라이드, 스포티지 
HEV 공급 확대로 3분기 미국 HEV 판매 +118%yoy 기록. EV 12.3만대
(+75%yoy), 서유럽 EV 판매는 +163%yoy 기록 
 4분기 전망: 중동 분쟁 지속으로 원가 부담의 급격한 개선은 어려울 전망. 
연초 CID에서 제시한 연간 판매 목표는 달성 가능할 것으로 보이지만 기존 
수익성 목표를 하회할 전망. 중장기적으로 상품 경쟁력 강화, 지역별 파워트
레인 대응 전략 유지, 내부 원가 및 체질 개선으로 외부 비용 부담 상쇄를 
추진할 계획. 중동 분쟁 완화에 따른 비용 부담 해소와 HEV 비중 확대를 통
한 수익성 개선은 2027년 시점이 될 것으로 예상 
 단기 방향 전환 어려울 듯: 판매 호조에도 불구하고 저가형 EV 판매 비중 
증가함에 따라 수익성이 악화되는 모습이 3분기에도 재확인. 글로벌 수요 
변화를 감안했을 때, EV 시장 선점이 매우 중요한 것은 사실이지만 단기 수
익성 부담 커지고 있는 점 우려됨. 중국산 EV의 글로벌 확산, 중동 전쟁 장
기화에 따른 원가 부담 증대가 연말까지는 영향을 미칠 것. 동사에 대한 투
자의견 매수와 목표주가 16만원 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율

## Document 244: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4 의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK 하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340 원에서 1,315 원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030 년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100 조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026 년 예상 FCF 의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

2026-10-29

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

## Document 281: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

## Document 284: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업.pdf

 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 
이지니

jini.lee@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중 
                                                               (단위: 십억원, %)

## Document 291: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

4 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.

the colour, the larger the change. The data series are defined as follows: Germany = new entries in the companies register (start-ups) since 2008; France = business start-ups, including

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 324: CCA macro and credit_ Stronger credits, more selective opportunities

Source PDF: all report pdf/CCA macro and credit_ Stronger credits, more selective opportunities.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 32
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CCA macro and credit November 2026

1

CCA macro and credit 
Stronger credits, more selective opportunities

Contents  
• Armenia  
(ARMEN) 
• Kazakhstan 
(KAZAKS) 
• Azerbaijan 
(AZERBJ) 
• Kyrgyzstan 
(KYRGYZ) 
• Uzbekistan 
(UZBEK)

The Caucasus and Central Asia (CCA) region has become a more resilient, better 
rated and more accessible market for investors. But, following a strong rally, 
improving fundamentals no longer guarantee attractive returns. In this report, we 
link sovereign ratings, strategy and macro resilience, while initiating coverage of 
Kyrgyzstan. 
Sovereign ratings: Improvement is increasingly priced in 
The CCA has stood out for its improving sovereign ratings despite a series of external 
shocks. Stronger balance sheets, economic reforms and greater political stability have 
supported upgrades and reduced perceived risk. But markets have moved ahead of the 
rating agencies: 5Y USD spreads already imply ratings at least one notch above current 
levels across the region. Further upgrades may therefore have less effect on bond prices, 
particularly in countries where spreads are already tight. 
Kazakhstan and Azerbaijan already have strong investment-grade profiles, leaving less 
room for further spread tightening. Uzbekistan remains our preferred sovereign credit, 
with reforms, fiscal discipline and privatisation creating room for further rating 
improvement and a broader investor base. Armenia looks more vulnerable to 
disappointment: positive rating outlooks sit alongside twin deficits and continued 
reliance on external inflows. Kyrgyzstan’s recent upgrade shows that the positive rating 
momentum is spreading beyond the region’s more established sovereign credits. 
Macro: Resilient, but increasingly divergent 
Our 2027 calls remain constructive on growth and currencies, but sources of resilience 
differ. High core rates are manageable while domestic real rates, sovereign buffers and 
capital inflows hold up, but a potential portfolio flow reversal is a risk. 
• Armenia: Benefits from strong inflows and progress in the peace process, although 
twin deficits and an expensive currency leave less room for disappointment. 
• Azerbaijan: Exceptional sovereign assets and high oil prices protect the peg, while 
weak oil production and limited diversification constrain growth. 
• Kazakhstan: Combines high real rates and better market access with persistent twin 
deficits and greater sensitivity to foreign portfolio flows. 
• Kyrgyzstan: Rapid infrastructure-led growth and an improving sovereign rating are 
offset by overheating, opaque external flows and growing state-led investment. 
• Uzbekistan: Combines high real rates, privatisation inflows and potential recovery of 
gold exports, but overheating and twin deficits prevent a structural appreciation call.

### Page 3

CCA macro and credit November 2026

3 
CCA macro and credit: Stronger 
credits, more selective opportunities 
This report serves two purposes. First, it follows publication of ING’s 2027 Global Outlook, 
which looks at how political, energy, technology and trade tensions could shape the 
global economy and markets over the coming year. Second, it continues our regular 
series of regional macro and credit reports, bringing together economists and sovereign 
strategists to assess the region from both a fundamental and an investor perspective. 
Our previous regional macro and credit report was published in March 2026, as the war 
in the Middle East was entering its early stages. We focused then on the sensitivities, ie, 
how the shock might impact on the region through commodity prices, global risk 
appetite, trade and imported inflation. Now, we are taking stock of how that resilience 
has held up and, more importantly, how the region is positioned for 2027. 
This edition also introduces a new format. As with our Directional Economics CEEMEA 
series, we complement the regular country coverage with a feature article examining a 
common regional theme. For this report, we focus on sovereign ratings. Ratings capture 
several of the changes taking place across the CCA; stronger sovereign balance sheets, 
institutional and structural reforms, better market access and, increasingly, the question 
of how much fundamental improvement is already reflected in asset prices. 
There are other common regional themes we could have explored. Capital market 
development and improving international access are becoming increasingly important, 
particularly in Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan. The interaction between commodity prices, 
currencies and capital flows is changing as foreign participation grows. Infrastructure 
investment and the expanding role of the state and SOEs are shaping growth and fiscal 
risks, while geopolitical fragmentation continues to redirect trade, remittances and 
financial flows. In the interest of focus, we leave these as themes for future editions 
rather than trying to address them all here. 
Lastly, the report marks two further changes to our regular regional coverage. We move 
from defining our research under the CIS (Commonwealth of Independent States), an 
intergovernmental political and economic organisation, to CCA (Caucasus and Central 
Asia) a geographic and economic grouping, bringing our terminology closer to the way 
the region is increasingly referred to internationally. And we add Kyrgyzstan, taking our 
core coverage from four to five economies. Its exceptional growth, infrastructure push, 
rapid financial deepening and improving sovereign-rating story make it a natural 
addition, while overheating and unusually complex external accounts provide plenty of 
questions for investors. 
The pages that follow bring these elements together through the ratings feature, a 
regional macro outlook and five country chapters.

Dmitry Dolgin 
Chief Economist, Russia and CCA 
dmitry.dolgin@ing.de

James Wilson 
EM Sovereign Debt Strategist 
james.wilson@ing.com

### Page 4

CCA macro and credit November 2026

4 
CCA sovereign ratings: The next leg 
of the upgrade cycle? 
The Caucasus and Central Asia region has absorbed a difficult combination of high global 
yields, commodity volatility and geopolitical fragmentation better than its frontier-
market label might suggest. However, resilience across countries has not been uniform. 
Armenia has relied on boosted external inflows, Azerbaijan on sovereign assets and 
energy prices, Kazakhstan on real rates and market access, and Uzbekistan on growth, 
gold and reform. But underlying these factors has been a general trend of improving 
macro fundamentals, reflected in sovereign rating upgrades. 
Our base case remains constructive, in terms of the outlook for further improvement in 
fundamentals, although upgrade momentum is gradually slowing. Set against this is the 
problem of relatively expensive valuations in the hard currency space, with credit 
spreads already tight compared to current rating levels. We would therefore be more 
selective in picking out opportunities, with Uzbekistan our favoured sovereign credit 
given potential for further underlying reform momentum. More broadly, the improved 
macro environment should be supportive for the wider investment case – improving 
credit quality can broaden international market access at both the sovereign, quasi-
sovereign and corporate level, allowing opportunities for increased issuance and offering 
a wider investor base. At the same time, reforms and macro stability should also 
continue to increase investor interest in local currency markets. 
The rating story so far 
In recent years, the Caucasus and Central Asia (CCA) region has been a standout among 
EM sovereigns in terms of improving credit fundamentals, with the countries seeing their 
reward of rating upgrades and reduced risk premium in market pricing. Azerbaijan, 
Armenia, Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan have generally each seen 1-2 notches in upgrades 
to their average credit ratings over the past five years or so. This has come despite the 
array of external shocks for the region, including the Covid lockdowns, Russia-Ukraine 
war, trade/tariff tensions and war in the Middle East. Some of the improvement can be 
ascribed to positive spillovers from the global story, including higher commodity prices, 
trade re-routing and financial inflows. But there have also been institutional and 
structural reforms, along with improved political stability, robust growth and stronger 
sovereign balance sheets. Outside of these four ‘core’ countries in our coverage, recent 
entrant into the EM debt universe, Kyrgyzstan, has also seen a rating upgrade to B2 this 
year by Moody’s, showcasing its potential for further catch-up.

Looking ahead, prospects are still skewed more positive (we have positive outlooks at 
Armenia, Azerbaijan and Uzbekistan), but we expect the momentum to slow. Uzbekistan 
looks like the country with the most potential to continue its upward trend, given the 
picture of structural reforms, while we are more sceptical regarding Armenia’s continued 
improvement, given some elevated external vulnerability and lingering political risks, 
despite it currently having positive outlooks at all three main agencies.  
Country level views  
Fig 2 CCA ratings by Moody’s, S&P and Fitch: An overview

Source: Moody’s, S&P, Fitch, ING

Kazakhstan: The nation’s rating profile has seen some modest improvement in recent 
years, with an upgrade by Moody’s to Baa1 in 2024 and to BBB by S&P this year. This has 
come on the back of resilient economic growth and gradual fiscal consolidation, despite 
signs of sporadic disruption to exports via the CPC pipeline.  
Large external assets and low debt provide a strong floor but the rating is constrained by 
inflation, commodity dependence, governance weaknesses and an uneven macro-policy 
framework. Rating upside would require firmer fiscal discipline, better coordination 
between fiscal and monetary policy, lower and more stable inflation, stronger 
governance and tangible diversification. The most relevant downside risks are sustained 
fiscal expansion, erosion of National Fund assets or disruption to key oil export routes. 
Our view is that the ratings should remain stable at current levels. Valuations in the hard 
currency space are already rich compared to rating levels, in particular with strong 
technical factors supporting front-end spreads. We would therefore expect lower 
investor sensitivity even in the event of further rating upside, although further ratings 
improvement may matter more for Kazakhstan's local market than for already-tight 
hard-currency sovereign spreads. High real yields, growing domestic issuance, stronger 
foreign participation and progress towards international settlement infrastructure are 
gradually broadening the potential investor base. This creates an important funding 
opportunity for the sovereign and quasi-sovereign sector, although greater foreign 
participation also increases FX sensitivity to global portfolio flows. 
Azerbaijan: Recent history has seen Azerbaijan upgraded to full Investment Grade at 
both Moody’s and Fitch amid signs of reduced reliance on hydrocarbons, improved 
institutional quality and progress in peace negotiations with Armenia. 
The sovereign balance sheet already compares strongly with investment grade peers, so 
further upside is less about debt reduction and more about the quality of the policy 
framework. The strongest triggers would be more predictable fiscal management, 
reduced reliance on sovereign wealth fund transfers, deeper monetary transmission, 
greater transparency and visible non-oil diversification. A durable peace process with 
Armenia would provide an additional institutional and geopolitical dividend.

### Page 6

CCA macro and credit November 2026

6 
We expect continued strength for the external and fiscal balance sheet, although see 
less likelihood of further rating upgrades beyond the current BBB- level. Recent years 
have seen decent progress on institutional categories within the World Bank WGI, such 
as government effectiveness, but corruption control and rule of law appear likely to 
remain, holding back further progress. 
We see diversification as the main constraint on further fundamental improvement 
rather than a near-term threat to creditworthiness. Large sovereign assets and 
continued hydrocarbon revenues provide substantial protection, but weak oil 
production, continued reliance on gas (despite broadening geography of gas exports) 
and subdued private investment leave potential growth around 2-3% in the absence of a 
stronger non-oil investment cycle. Further ratings should therefore depend increasingly 
on institutions and private-sector diversification rather than additional balance sheet 
accumulation. 
Uzbekistan: Uzbekistan has been upgraded by all three agencies in recent years and 
maintains one positive outlook, at Fitch. The upgrade cycle has been driven by 
institutional strengthening, rapid growth and fiscal repair, along with beneficial gold 
prices. 
The next leg depends on implementation; completion of energy subsidy reform, 
adherence to deficit and debt ceilings, tighter control of SOE and public-private-
partnership liabilities, credible privatisation and continued development of monetary 
and exchange-rate frameworks. Faster external borrowing or reform slippage would 
challenge the story. 
We can see the potential for the reform story in Uzbekistan to continue, although the 
next leg will depend increasingly on execution rather than headline growth. Continued 
fiscal restraint, SOE reform, privatisation and more disciplined management of 
contingent liabilities could support further rating convergence. Importantly for capital 
markets, these reforms are also generating a broader pipeline of sovereign and 
corporate issuance, potentially making Uzbekistan the region's deepest new-asset-
creation story. 
Armenia: Strong progress since 2022 has seen upgrades from B+ to BB-, with positive 
outlooks across the board. This has come on the back of easing political risks related to 
Azerbaijan, along with strong growth momentum and a sharp improvement in the 
government balance sheet due to increased capital and migrant inflows.   
Positive outlooks put the focus on whether strong growth can become broader and 
more durable. Upgrade evidence would include continued reform delivery, export and 
employment gains, fiscal consolidation that stabilises debt, lower borrowing costs 
through deeper domestic capital markets, and sustained normalisation with Azerbaijan 
and Turkey. Risks centre on renewed geopolitical tension, political disruption or a fading 
of post-2022 growth support without replacement by productivity-led investment. 
We view the picture as somewhat less positive, with persistent twin deficits and close 
economic ties with Russia important vulnerabilities. Russia accounts for around 80% of 
Armenia’s gas imports, one-third of Armenian foreign trade and roughly two-thirds of 
gross remittance inflows. Our base case remains gradual diversification rather than 
abrupt decoupling, but the continued reliance on Russian-linked flows helps explain why 
we are somewhat more cautious on further rating upside than the agencies' positive 
outlooks suggest. 
We expect ratings are more likely to stabilise at their current BB- level, although more 
genuine progress in normalising relations with Azerbaijan could offer some upside. We 
expect growth to moderate towards around 5% over the medium term, with the budget

### Page 7

CCA macro and credit November 2026

7 
deficit broadly around 3% of GDP. External deficits should remain sizeable enough to 
leave Armenia reliant on continued capital and remittance inflows. We view the 
composition of external inflows funding the nation’s current account as something of a 
vulnerability to watch and see current hard currency valuations as overly optimistic.  
Kyrgyzstan: The rating trajectory has turned positive; Moody’s upgraded the sovereign 
from B3 to B2 in October 2026, following a Positive outlook in 2025, citing rapid growth, 
higher income, improving diversification and a declining debt ratio. 
The next upgrade triggers would be a durable strengthening of reserves and the balance 
of payments, narrower structural current account deficits, continued fiscal discipline, 
higher per capita income and broader economic diversification. Downside triggers 
include disruption to Russia-linked trade or remittances, tighter sanctions enforcement, 
persistent off-budget support to SOEs and state banks, rising contingent liabilities, or 
renewed political instability.  
While the potential catch-up story to the rest of the region is of interest to investors, we 
expect further upside requires a more structural shift towards more sustainable growth, 
along with a reduction in structural external vulnerabilities. There also remains a clearer 
potential downside scenario compared to the rest of the region, given signs of economic 
overheating, large external vulnerabilities and increased borrowing needs for SOE capital 
injections. We therefore see the story as more balanced for now, despite the recent 
positive momentum.   
Why the rating story matters for markets 
While our base case is relatively optimistic for the fundamental and ratings trajectory 
for the region, this does not automatically translate into expectations for strong 
performance for hard currency sovereign debt in the region. Generally speaking, 
investors have moved ahead of rating agencies in terms of pricing in fundamental 
improvement. Current market pricing at five-year tenors in the dollar bond space implies 
ratings of at least one notch higher across the board, BB+ in Uzbekistan and Armenia, 
BBB in Azerbaijan and A at Kazakhstan. This suggests that further rating upside should 
have a more limited market impact in terms of spread tightening, in particular for 
Kazakhstan and Azerbaijan that are both already IG rated.

Source: Refinitiv, ING

In our 2027 macro outlook for the region (10 November 2026) we highlighted a potential 
upside scenario that could see markets start to price Uzbekistan like an Investment 
Grade credit, seeing demand broaden beyond traditional frontier and high-yield 
investors.

As a wider point, beyond simply expecting tighter credit spreads in the hard currency 
sovereign space, further upward momentum in the ratings space should still be 
supportive for widening the investor base for the CCA region, allowing more international 
issuance in the primary market at the sovereign, quasi-sovereign and corporate level. It 
should also encourage more investment on a broader scale (beyond hot-money and 
speculative portfolio flows), while continuing to increase investor interest in local 
currency markets. Kazakhstan has been placed on Index Watch-Positive for potential 
inclusion in JP Morgan’s GBI-EM local currency debt index and is currently part of the 
new frontier local-currency government bond index (GBI-EM Edge), while Uzbekistan has 
seen its soum-denominated sovereign international bonds included in the GBI-EM.

CCA macro and credit November 2026

9 
CCA macro: Resilient, but 
increasingly divergent 
The region’s improving sovereign ratings reflects genuine progress. But, as the preceding 
ratings discussion indicates, investors have already priced in much of this improvement. 
In our view, the key macro questions are what is underpinning the resilience of individual 
economies, how durable those supports are and where their trajectories are starting to 
diverge. 
Our 2027 macro outlook remains constructive on regional growth and currencies. This 
fits into ING’s global view of another potentially resilient year, despite continued power 
struggles around politics, energy, technology and trade. The CCA region has so far 
navigated this difficult global setting better than might have been expected, benefiting 
from strong domestic demand, commodity revenues, remittances, redirected trade and 
capital inflows. Positive real rates and generally strong sovereign buffers should continue 
to limit the transmission of higher core rates into regional currencies and monetary 
policy. Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan retain room for cautious rate cuts, Azerbaijan should 
remain broadly on hold, while Armenia and Kyrgyzstan face greater tightening risks. 
This resilience is not unconditional. Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan need to preserve 
sufficiently attractive real rates to support demand for local assets, while Armenia 
remains dependent on external inflows and Azerbaijan on hydrocarbon revenues. A 
reversal of portfolio flows would test currencies and protection from imported inflation, 
particularly where external or fiscal deficits remain sizeable. Geopolitics is another 
source of risk; tighter sanctions enforcement, greater scrutiny of regional trade and 
financial flows, or pressure from larger neighbours could reduce the benefits the region 
has drawn from fragmentation. 
The individual country stories increasingly reflect different combinations of these 
strengths and vulnerabilities. Armenia continues to benefit from remittances, tourism 
and capital inflows, but twin deficits, Russia exposure and an expensive dram leave less 
room for disappointment. Azerbaijan has the region’s strongest sovereign balance sheet 
and substantial protection from higher oil prices, although declining oil production and 
limited private sector diversification constrain medium-term growth. Kazakhstan 
combines strong sovereign assets, high real rates and improving international market 
access, but persistent twin deficits and growing reliance on portfolio inflows make the 
tenge more sensitive to global positioning. 
Kyrgyzstan, the newest addition to our coverage, represents the region’s most dynamic 
story. Infrastructure investment, construction, trade and rapid credit expansion have 
produced exceptional growth, stronger revenues and improving headline debt ratios. Yet 
double-digit growth increasingly resembles overheating; inflation is above target, real 
rates are low, monetary transmission is weak and external accounts remain difficult to 
interpret. Large trade deficits, dependence on Russia-linked remittances, opaque 
financial flows and growing state and SOE-related investment obligations mean that 
macro credibility still needs to catch up with headline performance. 
Uzbekistan remains our preferred medium-term growth and reform story. Privatisation, 
high real rates and capital inflows support the soum and the expanding investable 
universe. However, overheating, twin deficits and depleted liquid sovereign-fund buffers 
prevent us from turning near-term currency resilience into a structural appreciation call.  
The country pages examine these increasingly distinct growth, policy and external-
balance stories in greater detail.

Economic overview  
Armenia has remained resilient to the global and regional challenges of 2026, 
supported by renewed inflows of visitors, remittances and capital. Foreign-policy 
developments and solid finances provide additional support to sentiment and the 
balance of payments. However, this resilience increasingly relies on continued 
external inflows, while persistent twin deficits and close economic links with Russia 
remain important vulnerabilities. Strong domestic demand and external cost 
pressures have also pushed inflation above target and prompted the region’s first 
rate hikes this year.  
While we remain constructive on Armenia’s near-term macro outlook and assets, 
growing exposure to foreign political and flow-related risks, alongside an 
increasingly expensive AMD, reduces the margin for error. 
Economic activity supported by the new wave of external inflows  
GDP growth remains elevated at c.7% YoY on a 4Q rolling basis. Some softening in 
investment-heavy industries in 9M26 has been offset by stronger activity in 
consumption, financial services and real estate. Armenia is benefiting from additional 
visitor arrivals and financial inflows, with net remittances rising from a typical c.6% to 
c.8-9% of GDP.  
Around two-thirds of gross remittance inflows originate in Russia, making it a key 
variable for Armenia’s growth outlook. Assuming foreign policy continuity, gradual 
moderation of GDP growth towards c.5% pa, alongside a fiscal deficit, remains our base 
case. A material disruption to Russia-related flows of people, goods (including Russia’s 
parallel imports) and capital remains a downside risk and a factor to watch. The 
medium-term question is whether improving regional connectivity and Western 
investment can increasingly replace the exceptional Russia-linked inflows of recent 
years as a structural growth driver. 
Fig 10 Armenia: GDP growth components by production  Fig 11 Armenia: GDP growth vs remittances

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Household activity appears well supported by a combination of growing income, also 
thanks to remittances, fast lending growth and some rebound in state spending. 
Meanwhile, corporate activity is more subdued in 2026 compared to the 2025 spike as it 
remains heavily correlated with foreign trade and is subject to volatile regional politics. 
Russia still accounts for around one-third of Armenia's exports and imports, leaving 
corporate activity exposed to changes in bilateral relations.

Fiscal policy: Still supportive despite some recent consolidation 
The trailing budget deficit has narrowed to around 2.5% of GDP from 3.7% in the 
previous two years. Revenues have outperformed, mainly thanks to strong collection of 
consumption-related and corporate profit taxes, while spending growth has moderated, 
particularly in defence. We expect the deficit to gravitate back towards 3% of GDP over 
the medium term, but the commitment to overall fiscal discipline appears secure. 
Persistent deficits imply a steady issuance pipeline. We continue to expect the 
authorities to prioritise domestic borrowing to limit currency risk and develop local 
markets. Robust economic growth is improving debt sustainability despite rising nominal 
debt, leaving more room for potential external issuance as well. 
Fig 14 Armenia: Key budget parameters  Fig 15 Armenia: Key public debt parameters

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation and monetary policy: Growing CPI risks triggered a precautionary hike 
Inflation has hovered around 4% YoY for most of 2026. Relatively low import exposure to 
developed markets and c.5% YoY dram appreciation have helped contain price pressures 
from strong domestic demand and higher global commodity prices. Meanwhile, the 
deteriorating global inflation outlook, combined with regional and domestic risks, has 
raised concerns over further inflationary pressures among households, corporates and 
policymakers.  
With CPI likely to remain above the 3% target for longer, the Central Bank of Armenia 
made two consecutive 25bp hikes in September and November to 7.00%. We expect one 
further 25bp hike by year-end, although we do not see this developing into a prolonged 
hiking cycle.

Balance of payments and exchange rate: FX is supported, but increasingly expensive 
The dram has continued to show above-expected resilience to global capital volatility in 
2026, as it is supported by rising capital inflows, remittances, comfortable reserves and 
favourable investor sentiment linked to the peace process with Azerbaijan and positive 
rating trajectory. This continues to offset Armenia’s sizeable current account deficit, 
helping keep the currency stronger than implied by long-term valuation metrics.  
However, Armenia remains dependent on continued external flows, while close 
economic links to Russia remain vulnerabilities, even if the recent trade data points to a 
reduction in Russia-Armenia trade. Recent EU trade liberalisation covers around 80% of 
Armenian exports to the bloc. The immediate macro impact is likely to be limited, 
however, as the EU currently accounts for only around 12% of Armenia’s goods exports. 
We therefore expect the AMD to stay resilient to a potential global risk off in the near 
term but see limited room for further appreciation and continue to view longer-term 
risks as skewed towards gradual depreciation.

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Sovereign credit strategy 
This year has seen a continuation of fundamental improvement for Armenia, with 
positive rating outlooks at all three agencies, and elections that passed without major 
surprises. While there remains the potential for further positive catalysts from the 
ongoing peace process with Azerbaijan, conflict in the Middle East leaves Armenia in a 
more vulnerable position, given the increasing reliance on external inflows.

15

Economic overview  
Azerbaijan remains the CCA region’s financially strongest sovereign, supported by 
large and growing fiscal and monetary reserve assets, low public debt and a robust 
external balance sheet. Higher hydrocarbon prices have strengthened the near-
term fiscal and external outlook and supported confidence in the non-oil economy. 
However, the strength of the sovereign balance sheet contrasts with a much less 
impressive underlying growth story; declining oil production, limited diversification 
and subdued private investment continue to cap potential growth at around 2-3%. 
Inflation remains manageable for now, but imported price pressures warrant caution. 
We therefore remain constructive on sovereign resilience and AZN stability, while 
seeing the ability to convert hydrocarbon wealth into a deeper pool of private 
investment and investable non-oil assets as the main medium-term challenge. 
Economic activity: Recovering now, but long-term growth limited 
GDP growth is currently into the second year of below-average growth, dragged down 
by an underperforming fuel sector at the beginning of the year, reflecting both 
structural and temporary factors. At the same time, higher hydrocarbon prices support 
financial inflows, which pass through to the wider economy, supporting confidence in 
the non-fuel sectors. Retail trade and non-fuel industry output have been accelerating 
throughout 2026 amid faster lending activity.  
While the consolidated budget remains in surplus thanks to fuel revenues, the stable 
non-oil deficit supports domestic demand. Following the extended fuel-led slowdown of 
economic activity in 2025-26, we expect growth to recover in the medium term. 
However, the potential growth rate appears capped at 2-3% unless economic 
diversification accelerates. 
Fig 20 Azerbaijan: GDP growth by key contributors  Fig 21 Azerbaijan: GDP growth by sectors

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Household spending relies increasingly on retail credit, as positive real income growth is 
insufficient to cover consumption. On the corporate side, the fuel sector still weighs on 
industrial growth, but the recent rise in corporate lending suggests investment appetite 
elsewhere may recover.

Budget policy is benefiting from higher oil price environment 
Azerbaijan’s fiscal stance remains robust, though not growth restrictive. On the revenue 
side, fuel receipts are broadly stable, as lower export volumes are being offset by higher 
prices. Non-fuel revenues are also largely stable. On the expenditure side, higher social 
and defence spending, alongside persistently high investment outlays, continue to 
underpin spending pressures. The war in neighbouring Iran creates upside risks to 
defence and security spending.  
As a result, the consolidated budget remains in a comfortable surplus of around 2% of 
GDP thanks to fuel revenues, while non-fuel budget is in stable c.10% GDP deficit. 
Azerbaijan’s fuel fundamentals remain solid, with annualised revenues per US$1/bbl of 
oil price at US$150-200 million. This has helped keep the fiscal breakeven oil price stable 
at around US$59/bbl last year, even as spending has increased. Even assuming some 
increase in the fiscal breakeven in the coming years due to continued spending growth, 
ING’s house view on oil prices suggests that Azerbaijan will remain in fiscal surplus on a 
three-year horizon. 
For investors, the bigger issue is asset creation. Strong public finances limit sovereign 
issuance, leaving the investment story dependent on new non-oil projects and private-
sector borrowers.  
Fig 24 Azerbaijan: Consolidated budget balance  Fig 25 Azerbaijan: Budget sensitivities to oil prices

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation and monetary policy: CPI risks manageable, rates on hold 
Inflation risks have recently shifted somewhat to the upside, as food prices and higher 
global transport and commodity costs have renewed pressure on Azerbaijan’s producer 
price index. However, the exchange-rate peg remains a strong nominal anchor, limiting 
pass-through to consumer prices. With CPI still within the 4±2% target range, we expect 
the refinancing rate to remain on hold over the medium term.

However, further imported inflation linked to developments in the Middle East is the

Balance of payments and AZN: External buffers reinforced by oil 
As on the fiscal side, higher oil prices have materially improved the external outlook, 
reversing much of the deterioration seen in 2025. As each US$10/bbl of oil price adds 
annual exports of around 4% of GDP, in 2026F the current account surplus may reach 
12% of GDP. In the meantime, sovereign assets equivalent to 115% of GDP keep growing 
and continue to provide substantial protection against potential external shocks. These 
buffers remain the main support for the AZN peg and broader macro stability.  
Over the longer term, however, declining hydrocarbon production and limited export 
diversification are likely to gradually erode the external surplus. Azerbaijan remains one 
of the region’s strongest external stories, although commodity dependence continues to 
shape its medium-term outlook. 
Fig 28 Azerbaijan: 12M exports by region  Fig 29 Azerbaijan: External sovereign asset position

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Sovereign credit strategy  
The fundamental picture is generally positive for Azerbaijan, with ratings now 
comfortably in the IG space and Moody’s holding a positive outlook. At the same time, 
the country stands to be a significant beneficiary from the current energy price spike, 
given its high sensitivity to oil prices in terms of fiscal and external accounts. The 
ongoing peace process with Armenia seems more likely to act as a positive catalyst at 
this stage, given the progress in recent years.

### Page 18

18

Economic overview 
Kazakhstan continues to benefit from one of the strongest sovereign and external 
positions in the CCA region, supported by sizeable sovereign assets, a balanced 
network of international partners and higher oil prices. Strong portfolio inflows, 
attractive real rates, progress towards Euroclear connectivity, growing domestic 
debt supply and positive rating momentum have supported the tenge and local 
markets, while a sizeable backlog of future state FX sales provides an additional 
buffer against market stress. 
However, these strengths coexist with persistent current-account deficits, continued 
fiscal and quasi-fiscal stimulus, limited private sector investment and an uneven 
track record in fiscal discipline and inflation targeting. Greater reliance on portfolio 
inflows is also making the tenge more sensitive to changes in global risk appetite. 
This creates a growing trade-off between the benefits of deeper foreign 
participation in the local market and greater sensitivity to shifts in global 
positioning. Current inflation trends leave room for moderate monetary easing, but 
domestic fiscal and quasi-fiscal stimulus alongside external price risks should keep 
the easing cycle gradual. 
Economic activity: growth resilient despite oil disruptions and VAT hike 
GDP growth has slowed this year compared to the exceptional pace seen in 2025 but 
remains robust despite the VAT hike and oil-related disruptions. Disruptions to oil 
production and exports appear to have been the main drag on growth in 1H26 and 
remain the main risk for 2H26.  
Non-oil sectors continue to perform relatively well, while higher oil prices support 
confidence and improve the fiscal and external outlook. Domestic demand also appears 
supported by quasi-fiscal stimulus estimated at 4-5% of GDP. At the same time, retail 
and corporate lending are showing signs of cooling amid tight monetary conditions. We 
see GDP growth remaining in the 4.5-5.0% range in the coming years. 
Fig 30 Kazakhstan: GDP growth dynamics and structure  Fig 31 Kazakhstan: Key oil sector indicators

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Budget policy: Consolidation ongoing 
Headline fiscal policy has entered a consolidation phase, reflected in lower reliance on 
National Fund (NFRK) transfers to finance current spending, although a durable track 
record of fiscal discipline has yet to be established.

In addition to the quasi-fiscal stimulus provided through Baiterek lending, during 2026, 
the three-year state spending plan increased by KZT6.5tr to be carried out through 
targeted transfers from the National Fund (NFRK). This fits the previous pattern of 
spending plans being revised upwards over time. At the same time, even with the 
additional spending, the overall volume of National Fund spending, including guaranteed 
and targeted transfers, is still expected to decline, in our estimates, to 1.5-2.5% of GDP 
over the medium term.  
Importantly, however, this should have little effect on the headline republican (central 
government) budget deficit, which should remain in the 2-3% GDP range, determining 
the annual net debt issuance requirement. The consolidated budget should gravitate 
around balance but can also be subject to volatility due to the large role of NFRK’s 
investment income, which reached 4% of GDP in 2025. 
Fig 32 Kazakhstan: Republican budget balance  Fig 33 Kazakhstan: Consolidated budget balance

Inflation and monetary policy: Real rate to remain high 
The moderate easing in inflation from its 2025 peak and a resilient tenge have allowed 
the NBK to start cutting the base rate in 2026. Inflation pressures from the VAT hike and 
global commodity-price spike have remained under control for now, while portfolio 
inflows and attractive real yields have given an additional boost to the currency. At the 
same time, tariff increases, fiscal and quasi-fiscal stimulus, and producer-price pressures 
amid global inflation risks continue to justify our above-consensus CPI outlook.  
Therefore, we expect the easing cycle to continue only gradually, while remaining alert 
to potential external inflation shocks. Although Kazakhstan retains room for nominal 
easing, real policy rates are likely to remain high by regional standards and supportive 
of capital inflows.

Balance of payments and exchange rate: Portfolio flows increasingly matter 
Higher oil prices have strengthened Kazakhstan's external position, although part of the 
benefit is offset by weaker export volumes. Despite some recent improvement, the 
country continues to run structural current account deficits as strong imports and 
dividend outflows offset export revenues.  
Fig 36 Kazakhstan: Fuel export volume and value  Fig 37 Kazakhstan: Balance of payment composition

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

As a result, capital flows are becoming increasingly important for KZT dynamics. 
Progress towards Euroclear connectivity, positive rating momentum and additional 
public and corporate debt issuance should continue attracting portfolio inflows amid 
high real rates, supporting the tenge. Kazakhstan's capital market modernisation is also 
broadening beyond international settlement access, with the NBK now experimenting 
with tokenised debt issuance alongside the expanding digital tenge infrastructure. We 
have improved our KZT outlook but note that greater reliance on portfolio capital in an 
economy with persistent twin deficits also increases sensitivity to shifts in global risk 
appetite and flow reversals.  
The good news, however, is that the government has recently accumulated a backlog of 
FX sales linked to National Fund spending, which we estimate at c.US$2bn for June-
October. An unwinding of that backlog could provide an additional buffer for the 
domestic FX market in the event of external stress.

Sovereign credit strategy 
Overall, the underlying fundamental momentum is generally positive, with fiscal 
consolidation under close focus, while higher energy prices should offer a modest boost 
to export and budget revenues. However, as with budget policy, on the issuance front, 
more quasi-sovereign supply remains a risk in the coming years given low spread levels 
compared to most BBB peers.

Economic overview  
Kyrgyzstan is the newest addition to our CCA coverage and arguably the region’s 
most striking growth story. Economic growth remains exceptionally strong, 
supported by construction and trade activity, infrastructure investment, remittance 
inflows and rapid credit growth. Public finances remain relatively comfortable and 
international reserves have risen sharply, while the stability of the som has helped 
insulate the economy from external price pressures. 
However, the current pace of growth also points to significant overheating risks. 
Inflation is already well above the central bank’s target range, credit expansion 
remains rapid and real rates offer little evidence of tight monetary conditions, while 
the recent slowdown in corporate deposit inflows points to greater volatility in the 
corporate funding base. Large external deficits, dependence on Russia-related 
remittances and unusually opaque external flows further complicate the picture. 
The recent sovereign rating improvement is encouraging, but Kyrgyzstan remains 
an early-stage catch-up story in which macro credibility is still lagging the headline 
growth. 
Economic activity: Exceptional growth, increasingly demand-led 
Despite recent moderation to 9.7% YoY as of 3Q26, economic growth in Kyrgyzstan 
remains exceptionally strong at 12.3% YoY on a rolling four-quarter basis, with trade and 
construction alone contributing around 9.6 percentage points. Construction has become 
a significantly more important growth engine since 2022, supported by infrastructure 
investment, while strong trade activity continues to reflect both domestic consumption 
and Kyrgyzstan's role in regional transit trade. Industrial performance, by contrast, 
remains more volatile. 
The composition of growth makes the current double-digit pace unlikely to be sustained 
indefinitely. Some of the exceptional post-2022 boost from regional trade rerouting 
should gradually fade, while activity is increasingly reliant on domestic investment and 
consumption. Large infrastructure projects should keep growth relatively strong, but the 
challenge is to convert the recent acceleration into broader productivity gains rather 
than a prolonged demand boom.
